# Provenance

This repository has two kinds of code. The spec (`docs/baspowers/specs/2026-11-02-clean-room-client-design.md`, section 2) defines them.

## Summary

- **What:** this repository (`mindroom-ai/mindroom-chat`) is an Apache-2.0 web client for MindRoom, rebuilt clean-room from the legacy MindRoom Chat client (`mindroom-ai/mindroom-chat-legacy`), a fork of the upstream client (AGPL-3.0). On 2026-11-07 UTC the legacy repository was renamed from `mindroom-ai/mindroom-chat` to `mindroom-ai/mindroom-chat-legacy`, and that name passed to this repository; in text written before then, here, in `docs/provenance/` and in `docs/baspowers/`, MindRoom Chat and `mindroom-ai/mindroom-chat` mean the legacy client. Bas Nijholt's requirement (2026-11-03): no line of the upstream client's code in this repository, in any commit.
- **Lanes:** Lane P files were copied from MindRoom Chat `fc3a5905` in three waves; `git blame` credited at least 95% of each text file's lines to Bas, and every other line was blanked and written fresh. Lane C is everything else, written without the source of the upstream client or MindRoom Chat, as each session reports (appendix).
- **Grant:** Bas licenses the lines he wrote in the files of waves 1 and 2 under Apache-2.0 for this project. Third-party material in those files keeps its own license (`NOTICE`), and per his instruction of 2026-11-05 he licenses his MindRoom artwork among the wave 3 images (and the Android icons drawn from it) the same way.
- **Upstream overlap, 2026-11-05:** `scripts/upstream-overlap/check.py` finds 0 runs in the 2,805 tracked text files of main `64924634` and 0 in any blob of its 1,409 commits, against the upstream client's full clone at `76bbdc61` (all 86 refs). Its only 2 findings are in a local backup branch that main does not contain.
- **Similarity audit, 2026-11-05:** 0 clones with the upstream client; 49 with MindRoom Chat `fc3a5905`: 45 are Bas's own Lane P code, moved or repeated (mostly test setup), and 4 are trivial.
- **Clean-room rewrites:** 17 Lane P files that the ancestry audit of 2026-11-04 found derived from, or close to, upstream code were deleted unread and written again in Lane C (10 in round 1, 7 in round 2). The other files it flagged were deleted.
- **Tables,** all under `docs/provenance/`:

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| `portable.tsv`, `portable-wave2.tsv`, `portable-wave3.tsv` | the Lane P files of each wave, at their import-time paths |
| `import.txt` | the import commit of each wave |
| `holes.tsv`, `holes-wave2.tsv` | lines by other authors, blanked at import |
| `holes-overlap-import.tsv`, `holes-overlap.tsv`, `overlap.txt` | Lane P lines blanked for upstream overlap, in the import commit and at the tip of 2026-11-03 |
| `history-blanked.tsv`, `history-rewrite-commit-map.txt` | every line that the history rewrite of 2026-11-03 blanked, by blob, and its commit map |
| `dropped.tsv` | Lane P files deleted since their import, with the reason |
| `moves.tsv` | moved and deleted files: old path, new path, batch |
| `merged.tsv` | code merged or moved between files: old path and lane, new path and lane, commit, reason |
| `seams.tsv`, `seams-dropped.tsv`, `external.tsv` | the modules and packages that Lane P files import, and the seams that were dropped |
| `identifier-renames.tsv` | identifiers renamed in Lane P files |
| `rename-map.tsv`, `name-scrub.py`, `name-scrub-commit-map.txt` | the name scrub of 2026-11-04 |
| `audit/<date>/` | similarity audit reports (`clones.tsv`, `summary.tsv`) and their classification (`classification.tsv`) |
| `m1-baseline.txt` | starting error counts |

## Lane P: files ported from MindRoom Chat

- Source: the legacy client (`mindroom-ai/mindroom-chat-legacy`, then named `mindroom-ai/mindroom-chat`) at `fc3a5905` (`dev`, 2026-11-02), imported in three waves (wave 3: binary assets, `portable-wave3.tsv`, verified by blob hash).
- Selection: `git blame -w -M -C -C` attributes at least 95% of the file's lines to Bas Nijholt (`bas@nijho.lt`). Every line attributed to anyone else was blanked when copied and listed in the wave's hole file (path, line). Lane C rewrites the blanked lines. Blame credits whoever committed a line, so it also gave Bas lines that he committed but did not write: third-party material (the Capacitor native project templates and the Gradle wrapper scripts, the T3 Code minimap port, the sign-in marks, the code block icons and the context lines of the dependency patches; `NOTICE` names each with its license) and code that he had moved out of upstream files inside MindRoom Chat (see "Ancestry audit and clean-room rewrites").
- Wave 1: `docs/provenance/portable.tsv` (path, Bas lines, blanked lines): 1580 files, 329,093 Bas lines, 655 blanked lines in `docs/provenance/holes.tsv`. The TypeScript code and tests under `src/` and `e2e/`.
- Wave 2: `docs/provenance/portable-wave2.tsv`, same columns: 177 files, 73,719 Bas lines, 4 blanked lines in `docs/provenance/holes-wave2.tsv`. The other files Bas wrote: the native shells (`ios/`, `android/`), `patches/`, locales, test fixtures and traces, `public/` assets, `scripts/`, `serve.py`, and the Docker entrypoint.
- `docs/provenance/import.txt` has one line per wave: the import commit in this repository, then the source (`mindroom-chat fc3a5905`).
- App Store fixture avatars (`scripts/fixtures/appstore/avatars/`): copied from mindroom-ai/demo at `fc39fb8a`; Bas Nijholt confirmed on 2026-11-03 that they may be included under Apache-2.0 (`NOTICE`).
- Grant: per Bas Nijholt's instruction of 2026-11-02, he licenses the lines he wrote in the files of waves 1 and 2 under the Apache License 2.0 for this project (`NOTICE`). The grant covers only the lines he wrote: the third-party material above keeps its own license. Wave 3 is 61 images: 35 are the MindRoom logo artwork, which Bas licenses under Apache-2.0 for this project per his instruction of 2026-11-05, together with the Android launcher icons and splash screens that `scripts/generate-android-icons.sh` draws from it; the other 26 were the Capacitor template's Android images, replaced by those drawn icons and not covered by the grant.
- Tool: `scripts/port_files.py <chat checkout> <commit> [--wave N]` performs an import; `--verify` recomputes it and compares it with that wave's import commit. `scripts/verify-ported.sh <chat checkout>` (`just verify-ported`) runs the verification for every wave in `import.txt`.
- Holes: `scripts/check_holes.py --wave N --rev <commit>` (`just check-holes`) checks that each wave's files differ from the import only in their blanked lines, at the commit that filled them (`feed1b4` for wave 1, `ec7dca5` for wave 2). Lines blanked for Cinny overlap count as holes too (see below).
- Dropped: `docs/provenance/dropped.tsv` (path, wave, reason) lists the Lane P files deleted because their feature is not part of this client (Element Call, the room-level thread filter bar, Simple Mode), and since the restructure of 2026-11-05 also those deleted as dead code or merged into another file. They stay in the portable lists as imported: `check_holes.py` skips them, `verify-ported.sh` still verifies them (it reads the import commit, not the working tree), and the similarity audit counts them in neither lane.
- Removed from history: MindRoom Chat repository tooling (`.envrc`, `.github/`, `.claude/`, `.agents/skills`, `.gitattributes` with its merge driver, the release-tag and merge-driver scripts, the test of the release workflow) and `FORK_CHANGES.md` were imported in waves 1 and 2 and later deleted; the 2026-11-03 history rewrite removed them from every commit and their rows from the portable and dropped lists. `FORK_CHANGES.md` describes MindRoom Chat internals that were not ported, and no session may read it from any copy (see the session log for the sessions that saw parts of it before it was dropped).

## Lane C: clean code

Everything else. Each session reports in the appendix that it wrote its code without access to the source of the upstream client or MindRoom Chat beyond the Lane P files in this repository; the overlap check and the similarity audit check the code itself. Inputs are listed in the spec, section 2.

Supporting inventories, computed by the orchestrator from blame and imports only:

- `docs/provenance/seams.tsv`: modules that Lane P files import but that are not ported (path in MindRoom Chat, number of importing files, Bas share of the original, imported names). Lane C implemented them at those paths; since the restructure of 2026-11-05 they move like any Lane C module, and their Lane P importers are re-pointed with them. Rows that came from strings which only look like imports (fixtures of the import-parser tests, a comment, and two modules the e2e specs load inside the page) were removed on 2026-11-03.
- `docs/provenance/seams-dropped.tsv`: the same columns plus a reason, for seams (or single names of a seam) intentionally not built because their feature was dropped; their Lane P importers were deleted or no longer import them.
- `docs/provenance/external.tsv`: npm packages imported by Lane P files, with import counts.

`scripts/similarity-audit.sh <corpus>...` compares Lane C with each corpus: Lane P is both portable lists minus `dropped.tsv`; Lane C is every other file, tracked or untracked but not ignored, under `src/`, `scripts/` and `e2e/`, plus the root config files. A corpus that is a Git repository is compared as the files tracked at one commit (`<dir>@<commit>`, default `HEAD`, exported with `git archive`), so a checkout's untracked files and nested worktrees never count. Only the orchestrator, or a session it directs, runs it against the upstream client and MindRoom Chat, and only its report (paths and line ranges) is kept; "Similarity audit" below has the results.

## Name scrub

Bas's decision of 2026-11-03: the name Cinny appears only in this provenance record (`docs/provenance.md` and `docs/provenance/`), which needs it to describe the clean-room process. The rewrite of 2026-11-04 (`docs/provenance/name-scrub.py`, git-filter-repo) renamed it in the paths, file contents and messages of every commit after the signed initial commit `f31c1b1`, which is unchanged; every commit was kept, none squashed or pruned. Issue ids `CINNY-<n>` became `ISSUE-<n>`, e2e specs named after them got names that say what they test, `scripts/cinny-overlap/` became `scripts/upstream-overlap/`, storage keys and account data types got MindRoom names (`m.cinny.spaces` is `io.mindroom.spaces`, `cinny_*` keys are `mindroom_client_*`), and prose calls Cinny "the upstream client". In the record only paths and tool names changed; `docs/provenance/audit/` is untouched. `docs/provenance/rename-map.tsv` lists the path renames and the ordered rules; `scripts/port_files.py` applies it (`scripts/rename_map.py`), so every import wave still verifies against MindRoom Chat `fc3a5905` at its original paths. Commit hashes in `docs/`, `justfile`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md` and `NOTICE`, and blob ids in `history-blanked.tsv`, name the rewritten objects; `docs/provenance/name-scrub-commit-map.txt` maps each old commit to its new one, and the old commits are kept only in a private backup bundle. The output depends only on the input commits and the script, so a commit made on the old history after the rewrite is brought over by running the script with `--no-record` on that later history (its shared commits come out with the same hashes) and cherry-picking the rewritten new commits.

## Ancestry audit and clean-room rewrites

Blame decided Lane P, and blame credits the committer, so on 2026-11-04 a session audited the ancestry of every Lane P text file (1,757 files of waves 1 and 2): it followed each file's history in MindRoom Chat (`git log --follow`) and scored every version against every upstream version, keeping only paths, numbers and commit ids. 1,734 files had no upstream ancestry (class A). 16 had started from upstream code and were mostly rewritten since (B): 9 stubs of 1 to 14 lines at upstream paths and 7 others. 7 were still substantially similar (C), and 5 files of class A were borderline. None of these is in Lane P now:

- 17 were deleted unread and written again in Lane C from behaviour specs, one commit each, with the same exports. Round 1 (10 files): the 6 C files still in Lane P, 2 B files and 2 borderline files. Round 2 (7 files): the other 5 B files that were not stubs and 2 borderline files. Each has a `clean-rewrite` row in `moves.tsv` (empty new path) and a `dropped.tsv` row, "rewritten in Lane C from a behaviour spec (ancestry audit)"; the appendix logs each session.
- 11 were deleted: the 9 stubs (`restructure-p1-shims`), the borderline `usePendingThreadRoot.ts` as dead code (`restructure-p1-dead`) and the C file `CallEmbedHost.tsx`, dropped with Element Call on 2026-11-03.

31 files of class A share framework idiom above the audit's noise ceilings (folds components, vanilla-extract declarations, matrix-js-sdk listener hooks) and stay in Lane P, as the audit recommended. The audit also found the Capacitor template files and images that blame gave Bas; `NOTICE` names them, and the 26 template images were replaced.

## Moves

Bas's decision of 2026-11-04: the code is restructured into one layout, so files may leave the paths they were imported at. `docs/provenance/moves.tsv` lists every moved or deleted file, one row per file in the order of the moves: old path, new path (empty for a deleted file), batch. The restructure's move tool (`scripts/restructure/move.mjs`) wrote the rows. The restructure removed its tools when it was done (P4e); a later move takes them from the commit before that: `git worktree add /tmp/restructure-tools "$(git log --diff-filter=D --format=%H -1 -- scripts/restructure/move.mjs)^"`, then `node /tmp/restructure-tools/scripts/restructure/move.mjs --repo . --map <map.tsv> --batch <id>` in this checkout. `translate.sh` there carries a branch started at the old paths into the new layout, and `rename-identifiers.mjs` carries the renamed identifiers. The commit of a row is `git log --format=%h -S "$(printf '%s\t%s' <old> <new>)" -- docs/provenance/moves.tsv`.

- The portable lists, hole files, `dropped.tsv` and `seams.tsv` keep their paths. `verify-ported` reads the import commits and `check-holes` the commits that filled the blanks, where every file still sits at its listed path.
- Tools that look at the working tree carry each listed path through `moves.tsv` (`scripts/provenance-paths.cjs`): `strict-check.mjs`, the ESLint config, `similarity-audit.sh`, and `check_holes.py` without `--rev` (with `--rev`, it reads `moves.tsv` as of that revision).
- A move changes a Lane P file only in its import specifiers and the paths written in it; the move tool reports every such line. A Lane P file deleted as dead code or merged into another file gets a `dropped.tsv` row at its import-time path, and a deleted or merged seam a `seams-dropped.tsv` row.
- Batches: `clean-rewrite` (the 17 clean-room rewrites of "Ancestry audit and clean-room rewrites": 10 rows before the restructure, 7 after it), `restructure-p1-dead`, `restructure-p1-shims` and `restructure-p1-unwired` (deletions before the move), `restructure-p2` (the move itself), `restructure-p3-c<N>` (deletions, merges and renames of the cleanup chunks), `restructure-p4a` (renames left by the chunks) and `restructure-p4b-barrels` (the 51 barrels deleted and the 23 component folders they kept flattened). The P3 and P4 commit messages carry `Provenance:` lines for merges and for code moved between files.
- `merged.tsv` collects the `Provenance:` lines that merged or moved code between files: old path and its lane, new path and its lane, commit, reason (16 rows from 11 commits). In 7 rows Lane P code went into a Lane C file: 5 adapters inlined into their seam hosts and 2 test cases moved. A move does not change who wrote a line; the similarity audit can report such lines as Lane C clones of MindRoom Chat ("Similarity audit").
- The upstream overlap check reads contents, not paths; a moved file keeps its blob, and an edited one is checked like any new commit.

## Identifier renames

Decision 1 of the restructure (option A of its plan): `Mindroom` and `mindroom` leave the names of identifiers declared in Lane P files where they only marked the code as ours rather than upstream, as the file names did in P2. Product and protocol names keep them: the `MINDROOM_*` constants, `isMindroomAgent*` and `hasMindroomAgents`, Local MindRoom, the MindRoom homeserver checks, the `MindRoom*` native plugin and brand names, the MindRoom docs URL, config keys, storage keys, persisted IndexedDB names, the `window.__MINDROOM_*` globals and the `mindroomUi` i18n namespace.

- `docs/provenance/identifier-renames.tsv` lists every renamed identifier: old name, new name, kind (`export`, `local`, `import alias` for the local names of renamed Lane C exports, `file name` for an export named after its file, and one local renamed to free a name).
- `scripts/check-identifier-renames.mjs` (`just identifier-renames`, part of `just check`) proves that every Lane P line a commit of that table changes differs from the line before only by the names the commit adds; a revert that removes rows is checked with the names the other way round. Strings count too: test titles, one error message and one architecture test's source check name renamed identifiers.

## Upstream overlap check

Bas's requirement of 2026-11-03: no line of Cinny's (AGPL-3.0) code in this repository, in any commit. `scripts/upstream-overlap/check.py --corpus <Cinny git repository>` (`just upstream-overlap <corpus>`, part of `just check`, skipped without a corpus) compares files with every version of every Cinny file in the corpus's history and prints only paths and line ranges of this repository. It flags:

- **Line runs:** 3 or more consecutive counted lines that each occur in Cinny. A counted line has at least 8 characters after trimming and is not trivial. Trivial lines are skipped (they neither count nor break a run): import and re-export statements (the ported files keep Cinny's module paths), JSX closing tags, JSX tags or attributes whose values are all literals (folds design tokens such as `variant="Surface"`), lines that are only a Matrix namespaced identifier (`'m.relates_to': {`), lines that are only a name or pass a prop under its own name (`eventId,`, `room={room}`; the ported code fixes these names), and comment lines. Lines that also occur in third-party code under `node_modules` are skipped as generic. `package.json` and `package-lock.json` are not checked (dependency names and versions).
- **Token runs:** 30 or more consecutive tokens of JavaScript, TypeScript or CSS (comments, imports and trivial lines left out) that occur in the same order in a Cinny file.

`--history` checks every blob of every commit. jscpd at 30 tokens against six Cinny release snapshots (`--jscpd`) was used in addition for the rewrite; its ranges are coarser.

Since 2026-11-05 the pre-push hook `scripts/git-hooks/pre-push` (`just install-hooks <corpus>`, AGENTS.md) runs the check before each push on every commit the push adds, each file as that commit has it (`check.py --pre-push`), and refuses the push on any finding, a missing corpus or a checker error. A later commit that deletes the lines does not clear the commit that added them.

History rewrite of 2026-11-03 (`scripts/upstream-overlap/rewrite_history.py`, git-filter-repo; the commits and their messages stay, one commit that became empty was pruned, and the history still starts at the repository's initial commit):

- Every flagged range in every blob was replaced by empty lines (imports kept, line numbers unchanged), and each blob was checked again and blanked further until it passed. `docs/provenance/history-blanked.tsv` lists every blanked line by blob. The rewrite ran three times on 2026-11-03: once over the history up to the M1 branch, once to drop a removed file's row from `portable.tsv`, and once over the fill commits below after the check learned the name-only and comment rules.
- Lane P lines blanked in the import commits are listed in `docs/provenance/holes-overlap-import.tsv` (path, line, wave); `port_files.py --verify` blanks them too, so every wave still verifies against MindRoom Chat `fc3a5905`.
- At the tip (`docs/provenance/overlap.txt`), 5,854 lines in 355 files were blank. Lane C sessions wrote them fresh without the removed code (round 1), then rewrote what still matched, with the check's line ranges for their own files as feedback (round 2). The 821 Lane P lines among them (84 files, `docs/provenance/holes-overlap.tsv`) were filled one line each; `check_holes.py --overlap --rev 54314f8` checks that.
- The 821 Lane P lines are lines that `git blame` credited to Bas but that the check found in runs shared with some version of the upstream client (3 or more lines, or 30 or more tokens). The wave 1 import commit holds 818 such blanks in 68 files (`holes-overlap-import.tsv`, which `port_files.py --verify` applies too); the two lists differ in 34 files, 32 of which changed between the import and the tip. None of these lines is in any commit now: the rewrite blanked them everywhere, each was written fresh, and the run of 2026-11-05 below finds nothing reachable from main. They showed that blame alone can credit Bas with lines that match the upstream client; the ancestry audit of 2026-11-04 then looked for such derivation file by file.
- `docs/provenance/history-rewrite-commit-map.txt` maps each commit from before the rewrite to its new commit. The old commits exist only in a private backup bundle.
- Result on 2026-11-03: 0 findings at the tip and in every blob of every commit.
- jscpd at 30 tokens against the six snapshots, at the tip after the rewrite: 0 clones in Lane C; 7 ranges in 3 Lane P files Bas wrote (`ConnectPage.tsx`, `MindroomConnectionsSettings.tsx`, `CompactRoomView.tsx`), made of folds JSX tags and attributes with literal values (exempt above) and at most 2 lines that also occur in Cinny. Their lines were not changed.

### Run of 2026-11-05

- Repository: main `649246345ce0`, GitHub's only branch (1,409 commits), checked from a clone whose other refs were older work branches and a local backup of the old main.
- Corpus: the upstream client's full clone, HEAD `76bbdc6130b7` (`v4.12.8` and 11 commits), all 86 refs. The release tags that `--jscpd` would export (not run this time): `v1.8.2` `12369ba2`, `v2.2.6` `f6694031`, `v3.2.0` `9ecb2337`, `v4.0.0` `96415a8d`, `v4.10.2` `c88cb4bc`, `v4.12.8` `1e6412ed`.
- At HEAD: 0 findings in 2,805 tracked text files (21:39 to 21:48 UTC, together with the history run).
- `--history`: 10,471 text blobs of every ref, 10,422 of them reachable from main. 2 findings, both line runs in blob `038167ec` (`src/app/mindroom/threads/ThreadContextBanner.tsx`, lines 493 to 496 and 538 to 541). The blob enters in commit `0f4b2a72` and is replaced four commits later in `8854f143`, all on the local branch `old-main-pre-restructure-20261105`, a backup of main from before the restructure that was never pushed. `git merge-base --is-ancestor` exits 1 for both commits against main, and the blob is not among the objects reachable from main, so no finding is reachable from main.

## Similarity audit

`scripts/similarity-audit.sh` (jscpd 4, at least 50 tokens) compares Lane C files with each corpus. Since 2026-11-05 a corpus that is a Git repository is compared as the files tracked at one commit; before, the script read a corpus directory as it was on disk. Reports keep paths and line ranges only, no code. jscpd reports each duplicated span once, against one of the files that hold it, so a change of corpus can move a clone to another file.

| Run | Upstream client | MindRoom Chat | Report |
|---|---|---|---|
| before rewrite (2026-11-03) | `v4.12.8` checkout: 52 (694 lines) | `fc3a5905`: 129 (1842 lines) | `docs/provenance/audit/2026-11-03-before-rewrite/` |
| after rewrite (2026-11-03) | 0 | 16 (182 lines) | `docs/provenance/audit/2026-11-03/` |
| after the restructure and the clean-room rewrites (main `9d9848bc`, 2026-11-05), corpora read from disk | the full clone's working tree: 0 | the checkout with its `.worktrees/`: 48 (597 lines), 46 of them against files under `.worktrees/thread-components-refresh/` | `docs/provenance/audit/2026-11-05-with-worktrees/` |
| rerun at main `64924634` (2026-11-05), tracked files only | the full clone at `76bbdc61` (852 files): 0 | `fc3a5905` (2,911 files): 49 (610 lines) | `docs/provenance/audit/2026-11-05/` |

None of the 135 flagged spans before the rewrite was explained by a Lane P file in this repository, so all were rewritten from their file and line ranges alone. After the rewrite, 15 of the 16 MindRoom Chat clones are new Lane C tests that mirror the setup of ported Lane P tests present in this repository (the matched MindRoom Chat files are Lane P here), and the last one is the dependency list in `package.json` (package names and versions).

`classification.tsv` beside each MindRoom Chat `clones.tsv` of 2026-11-05 classifies every clone, from paths, line ranges, the provenance lists and this repository's files only (no MindRoom Chat file was opened): the lane of the matched MindRoom Chat file (portable lists, `seams.tsv`), whether its range holds a blanked line (hole files), and a jscpd run of the Lane C file against that Lane P file where it is now. The rerun reports 47 of the first run's 48 clones again, plus 2 more; the first run's other clone matched a MindRoom Chat file version that `fc3a5905` does not have.

| Class | Rerun | First run | What it means |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lane P code | 45 | 43 | Bas's own Lane P code: moved into the Lane C file (3, `merged.tsv`), the same code as in that Lane P file here (40 in the rerun: 38 test setup and 2 small promise helpers), the folds mock of a Lane P test (1), or class names and behaviour that a Lane P test fixes (1 in the rerun, `Reasoning.tsx`; the matched file is the seam's original, 94% Bas by blame) |
| trivial | 4 | 5 | the options types that two clean-room rewrites kept for their callers (3), and folds layout tokens around Lane P catalog keys (`Profile.tsx`; in the first run also `LogoutDialog.tsx`, against a MindRoom Chat version on another branch) |
| generated | 0 | 0 | |
| needs action | 0 | 0 | |

Two lines of one kept interface were blanked at import as not Bas's (`roomFocusScrollController.ts` lines 67 and 68 at `fc3a5905`). In the rewrite they declare `scrollToElement` and `scrollToItem`, names that its callers pass, and the overlap check finds no upstream run there.

## What the orchestrator saw

The orchestrating session (Claude Opus 5.5, 2026-11-02) ran MindRoom Chat `fc3a5905` locally and captured `docs/design/reference/`; computed blame and import graphs over MindRoom Chat; read MindRoom Chat repository metadata (license, package manifest, path names, settings labels in the running app); and read the MindRoom backend docs and source (Apache-2.0). It never opened a Cinny-authored source file. On 2026-11-03 it built the overlap check, which reads a full Cinny clone; it looked at lines of this repository that the check reported (to classify trivial lines and tune the rules) but not at Cinny's files.

The overlap check and the similarity audit check the code itself.

| 2026-11-06 | scaled pictures kept offline (offline-scaled-media) | Claude Opus 5.5 | Bas's decision: since `1038b163` the timeline shows an unencrypted PNG or JPEG without a sender thumbnail as the homeserver's 800x600 scaled copy, and nothing saved it, so a picture seen online after Download entire room showed a spinner offline (offline-room-content "server edit" failed on main). | 2026-11-06 | Reply counts after a reload and while a thread opens (thread-count-flush) | Claude Opus 5.5 | `e2e/threads/thread-reply-counts.spec.ts` failed on main now and then; main-red-specs read it as the cross-room index publishing a count halfway through a sync batch. Traced in the built app (console trace of every thread update, index flush, root summary write and card text, gce-dev and macbook-m5): no batch publishes a halfway count. The Threads page's first count after a reload is the saved sync's, which the client saved at a redaction and not after the newer reply, and the server's count follows. The spec's last step now expects the saved count or the server's, then the server's, never any other. That step and others in the spec also caught a card showing more replies than the thread has. There were three causes, each fixed with a test that fails first: (1) a reply that sync brings while its thread opens was counted twice, because the root the opening thread had just read already counted it, and an opened thread is never corrected (`keepThreadReplyCounts` now settles a thread's count as each event lands in its timeline, after the SDK's own listener); (2) the event cache took a redacted latest reply out of a cached root's thread summary but kept its count, and the partial merge (larger count wins) raised the live root (`stripRedactedRelationsFromRawEvent` lowers the count too; Lane P edit by Bas's direction); (3) `readOnlyEventMapper` let the SDK's mapper write a cached root's older summary into the room's own root (it now keeps the room's summary). Not fixed, described in the report: a Compact room reloaded after a non-latest reply was redacted and a newer one arrived starts at one reply too many, because the cache restore adds the newer reply to a cached count that predates the redaction (event cache design). The spec passes 10 of 10 per browser on gce-dev, macbook-m2 and macbook-m5, apart from two runs that failed at sign-in or skipped on load. |

Five causes, each with a unit test that failed first. (1) Cache hydration compared the cached reply's edit time with the card's reply time, which a title previewed for want of a reply supplied, or with the live activity: a title sent after the reply finished always won. Now a previewed title notice has no reply time, the activity time stands in for the cached reply only in the bundle-or-cache choice, and hydration compares reply with reply only when the card shows a dated reply, else activity with activity as before (`threadPresentation.ts`, `threadRecord.ts`, `threadOverviewCacheHydration.ts`, Lane P, Bas-directed edits). (2) A root keeps the `io.mindroom.latest_reply` of the moment it was fetched: once the device knows a newer thread event or a newer revision of the bundle's own `latest_event`, the bundled reply is ignored (`threadBundledPreviews.ts`, `threadPresentation.ts`). (3) A restored root whose summary the cache moves to a newer event drops the server's MindRoom previews (`cachedThreadSummary.ts`). (4) Merging two saved copies of a root kept the fresher `latest_event` but every other summary key from the older copy, pairing the title with the first read's placeholder; the previews now travel with the latest event they came with (`eventRevision.ts`, Lane P, Bas-directed edit). (5) Hydration skipped a cached reply equal to the one the card showed; a card can show it from its root's bundle while another copy of that root, from the room timeline, carries an older bundle, so that card kept the placeholder. The cached reply and sender are now stored whenever the cache is chosen (`threadOverviewCacheHydration.ts`, Lane P, Bas-directed edit). Found with logging builds of the presentation inputs on a test runner (not committed). thread-cache-reload ten times per engine at the final commit: on the Linux runner 20 of 20 against the runner's previous Tuwunel image (the carry's setup; the commit before these fixes failed WebKit 2 of 2) and 19 of 20 against v1.9.4-mindroom.29, the miss a room that did not open within a minute; on a Mac runner under other load 13 of 20, every failure an off-screen card still on the placeholder or a step text in the live phase, which main shows the same way there (main 34718395: 13 of 17 before its run stopped). | 2026-11-06 | Jump to Unread in WebKit (jump-unread-webkit) | Claude Opus 5.5 | `e2e/threads/threads-view-unread-line.spec.ts` people-room part: Jump to Unread left the New Messages line out of view, mostly in WebKit and now and then in Chromium. The spec had scrolled on by itself and checked only that the line was rendered; it now waits for the view to rest and requires the whole line in the timeline's visible band (failing first: WebKit 16 of 60 runs, Chromium 2 of 40, on macbook-m5, macbook-m2 and gce-dev). Traced in the built app (every scroll write with its stack, rows, edge sentinels and requests per frame): never a WebKit scroll-anchoring or measurement-order fault. The read marker was older than the loaded rows, so the jump asked /context, whose SDK call takes no neighbours, and the room showed the marker's row alone at the bottom of the view; the focus jump could not aim it, ended, and the pages that loaded afterwards kept it there, the line under it below the visible rows. When a page of the previous rows was still out at the press, it landed after /context and the SDK linked the event's timeline to the live one behind the window, which kept its single row with no edge sentinels. Fix: the event loader (`timeline/timelinePaginationController.ts` `loadRowsAround`) brings a page on each side whenever the chain around the event holds at most one row, before the jump; unit test fails first. After: 0 failures in every run that reached the jump (WebKit 63, Chromium 34). No WebKit-specific change was needed. |

Failing-first tests: `e2e/threads/thread-scroll-stability.live.spec.ts` (all four configurations fail on main) and unit tests. Found, not fixed: the Compact view rebuilds every thread record per event (the loaded-machine jitter), Threads view rows still move 22 to 31 px as unmounted rows above change their estimates and reshuffle when an older page loads, and the Threads view part fails in Chromium's phone emulation. | 2026-11-07 | The Inbox user ID check under load (impostor-notify-name-flake) | Claude Opus 5.5 | "the Inbox shows the user ID after a copied name in an unopened room (WebKit, phone)" failed once in a loaded run on gce. Diagnostics on gce reproduced it 6 of 6 times with a 1.5 s pause after the spec's scroll: the copy's user ID stayed laid out whole inside its row, but the row had been pushed below the viewport (row at 691 to 811 right after `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded`, 811 to 1005 later, viewport 844). The Inbox lays out only rows near the viewport (`content-visibility: auto`, 120 px placeholders against 114 to 194 px rendered); the rows the scroll brought near took their real height a frame later, and WebKit keeps no scroll anchor. The check passed only when it ran before they rendered. No clipped frame in the app, so the spec changed: `showsUserIdWhole` scrolls the row to the viewport's center itself and measures only once the row's box is the same in two animation frames, else the poll tries again. A scratch run against the pre-wrap row (one truncated line) then showed a second weakness: WebKit reports a range in ellipsized text only as far as the ellipsis, so on gce the cut-off ID's visible "(@i" looked whole (on m5 it gave a zero-width rectangle and failed). The check now also requires that no element between the ID and its row that clips its overflow holds more than fits (`scrollWidth`, `scrollHeight`); it fails on the pre-wrap row in both engines. No app or Lane P file changed. |

(2) The incoming call ring had no call membership behind it, and `IncomingCalls` stops a ring whose call is empty at its next one-second check, so the Ignore click raced the card's removal and waited for the whole test timeout. With 2.5 s added before the click the old spec failed in both engines. The seed lets members send call memberships, the member joins the call before ringing, and the click gives up after 30 s. A probe also showed the spec's `pushState(null)` navigation gives every page React Router's key `default`; the drawer still followed it correctly. Spec only; no app change. |
